## Document 029: 20261023【投研報告】市場快訊_AI資金壓力拖累科技類股，惟產業需求仍具韌性

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少， 道瓊工業指數上漲0.02%，收52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓 ， 資金流向非科技股及公債 ：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。

二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負 ，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力 ：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視 ， 以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力 。 後續10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲 ， 顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股 ：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象 。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%， 第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比 ， 反映目前市場主要擔憂AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業 ， 尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散 ：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高 。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察 ， 中期仍看好具基本面支撐的AI 產業 ：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡 。 投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性 ， 中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

個股簡評 
高階銅箔基板產能供不應求，載板可望通過
驗證並成為新成長動能，以上將帶動產品組
合持續大幅優化，推升報價和毛利率，讓今

業務營收比重

公司簡介  
台光電子材料股份有限公司成立於 1992 年，
初期主要供應  FR-4 銅箔基板與膠片；並於  
2013 年 ， 成為全球最大無鹵素基板供應商 ， 迄
今仍在市場保持領先地位 。 台光電持續開發出
各種優良的無鹵素新產品，從  Mid. Loss 、 
Low Loss、Very Low Loss、Ultra Low Loss 到 
Extreme Low Loss 等不同等級材料 ， 並符合各
種 高 精 密 先 進 PCB 技 術 ， 如Anylayer 、
mSAP、封裝載板、超高層板、高速傳輸及高
頻產品等各應用場域之需求。

2

營運展望 
1. 3Q26 需求強勁及漲價效應帶動營收獲利續創歷史新高：高速基材需求強勁、
帶動產品組合持續優化、及全品項漲價效應顯現，第三季營收續創歷史新高，
亦同時帶動毛利率進一步上揚。台光電第三季營收 609 億元、+28.8% QoQ、
+142.1% YoY， 毛利率 37.7%。 營業利益 187 億元、+46.8% QoQ、+274.5% YoY，
營業利益率 30.7%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 145 億元，每股盈餘 40.47 元。期
末存貨總額提升至 271 億元，細項中原物料達 178 億元 、 製成品約 70 億元 、
在製品約 22 億元及商品約 7,774 萬元 ； 增備存貨主要著眼於高階原料市場吃
緊，藉由提前拉高原料水位以保障後續生產。 
2. 4Q26 需求仍然強勁將延續前幾季成長動能 ：第四季受惠產品結構與定價優勢 ，
將延續全產全銷強勁營運態勢，預估整體營收可望季增兩成。毛利率預期也因
定價優勢可進一步明顯提升。整體成長動能涵蓋 AI 伺服器、通用伺服器、高
階交換器、低軌衛星及記憶體模組等多重領域。高階基材整體價格持續調漲，
全產品線綜合漲價幅度預期落在 5 ~ 20% 之間 。基礎設施延續漲價效應及產品
組合優化將季增兩成，行動裝置因需求呈現持平，車用與工業則因產能優先挪
往高毛利產品，營收受排擠將季減約一成。 
3. 載板近期可望通過驗證並成為新一波成長動能：硬質銅箔基板涵蓋高密度互連
材料 (High Density Interconnect, HDI)、高速材料 (High Speed Digital, HSD) 與
載板 (Substrate) 三大範疇，雖然日韓對手高階 HDI 技術與台光電相仿，但擴
產停滯且侷限本土生產；台光電憑藉靈活量產規模與成本優勢，競爭力顯著勝
出。為追求極速傳輸與超低延遲，AI 系統架構加速走向模組化設計。單板晶片
密度倍增直接帶來線寬與線距極限微縮、高層數壓合等多重嚴苛的高階製程考
驗。台光電整合 HDI、高速與載板技術，推動「HDI 載板化」 ，確保板材歷經
多次高溫壓合仍具備載板級穩定性與可靠度。 
4. 產品組合持續往高階低損耗優化，新產能長約綁定將全產全銷：高階超低損耗
材料滲透率維持上升格局，M7+ 營收占比已從去年 50 ~ 55% 提升至 65 ~ 70%，
隨著 M9 下半年步入量產 ， 明年出貨規模放大 ， 佔比將進一步墊高至 70 ~ 75%。
產能也將自目前 585 萬張擴充至 2030 年 1,525 萬張。面對高階銅箔基板供
需日益緊俏且合規供應商有限下，終端客戶近期主動要求簽署長期供貨協議。 
5. 產品組合優化報價毛利率攀升，獲利將再創歷史新高：高階銅箔基板產能供不
應求，帶動產品組合持續大幅優化，推升報價和毛利率，今明年營運獲利將續
創歷史新高。 合庫預估 2026 年營收 2,086 億元、+121.3% YoY， 毛利率 35.3%。
營業利益 591 億元、+209.4% YoY，營業利益率 28.3%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 
457 億元，每股盈餘 127.17 元。 
6. ESG (台光電在 2026 年上市公司治理評鑑結果為 6 ~ 20%)：台光電子針對各

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因

元。業外收入約貢獻 EPS 3.6元。 
➢ 26Q4 財測營收 2,101~2,218 億元(以美元兌台幣匯率
31.7 估計)，QoQ+25.0%~+32.0%。毛利率、營業費用率
中位數為 45.0%、26.0%。雖然 26Q4 消費性電子季節性
需求較低，但因為 AI AISC放量，26Q4 營收大幅季增。 
➢ 上修 26Q4 資料中心 ASIC營收預估到 25億美元(前次預
估 20億美元以上)，主要因為客戶需求強勁，且爭取到供
應鏈額外產能如 ABF。先前預估2027 年可服務市場規模
(SAM) 800億美元，市佔目標15~20%，本次預估市佔目
標達到高標。下一代 2nm ASIC專案預計 2028 年量產。
第 2家雲端資料中心專案 design kickoff，預計2028 年貢
獻營收。

營 運 分 析

聯發科為全球前五大 IC設計公司 
聯發科是台灣最大 IC設計公司，也是全球前五大無廠半導體(fabless)公司，
成立於 1997/05，並於 2001/07 掛牌上市。在無線通訊與數位媒體領域布局
多年。26Q3 三大產品線分為 Mobile Phone 37%、Smart Edge Platform 
58%、Power IC 5%，Mobile Phone產品以手機應用程式處理器(Application 
Processor)為主；Smart Edge Platform產品包括路由器、寬頻網路、藍芽耳
機等各種有線無線晶片、ARM架構運算單晶片、智慧電視、數位機上盒，以
及客製化晶片服務。

產，例如5G thin modem 導入新手機客戶，以及 TV晶片中反應 DRAM 漲價
貢獻。Power IC 營收 QoQ+1%，YoY+18%，年增來自資料中心相關業務。

增。

圖四、26Q4 AI ASIC放量 
聯發科各季營收及獲利走勢預估 (百萬元)

資料來源：聯發科；兆豐投顧預估

26Q4 ASIC上修，2027年 ASIC營收達到預估高標 
聯發科上修 26Q4資料中心 ASIC營收預估到 25億美元(前次預估 20億美元
以上)，主要因為客戶需求強勁，且爭取到供應鏈額外產能如ABF。雖然資料
中心 ASIC 的毛利率略低於公司平均，但因為供應鏈成本上升，漲價反應，
且資料中心業務的營業費用率低，營益率高於公司平均，26Q4 起加分效益
顯現。 
先前預估 2027 年可服務市場規模(SAM) 800 億美元，市佔目標 15~20%，
本次預估市佔目標達到高標。下一代 2nm ASIC專案預計 2028 年量產。 
第 2家雲端資料中心專案 design kickoff，預計 2028 年貢獻營收。公司表示
非中國客戶。 
技術佈局方面，已提前投資 Ayar Labs 佈局光通訊、CPO相關研發。448G 
SerDes已 tape out。 
26Q3 完成 39 億美元海外 CB 發行，NVIDIA、Alphabet 皆參與認購，有助
於未來 AI生態系合作。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

What surprised us 
AirTAC reported 3Q26 results today (October 29th). 3Q OP was in 
line with our estimates, but was 5% higher than BBG consensus. 
OPM was 33.9% in 3Q26, largely in line with our estimates; although 
GM of 49.4% was higher than our estimates by 0.9ppt, it was offset 
by higher opex (NT$1,778mn vs. GSe of NT$1,669mn). Total 
revenue was largely in line with GSe but was 4% above consensus, as 
the company enjoyed solid demand from the electronics industry 
(33% YoY in 3Q26 vs. 20% YoY in 2Q26, which accounted for 28% of 
total 3Q26 revenue). 
What surprised us the most during the results call was the 
company’s solid guidance on industry growth (mentioned low single 
to mid single digit % YoY growth is sustainable for the longer term 
for the China pneumatic market) and the company further 
mentioned that it could outgrow the market by 10ppt YoY in 2027. 
Moreover, the company expects all 
electronics/automotive/machine tools/general 
machinery/textile/packaging to see double digit YoY growth in both 
2026/27, thanks to the China government’s ongoing stimulus 
policies. Management reiterated 2026 revenue guidance in Rmb 
terms to reach 20%+ YoY growth (could reach higher YoY in NT$ 
terms), and maintained 2026 OPM guidance of 34%.  
The company reiterated its utilization rate target for its linear guide 
business to reach 50% by 2026 end (vs. 40%+ in 3Q26). The 
company continues to see peers raise prices for their linear guide 
products, and management thinks the company is well-positioned 
to gain market share with its stable pricing and shorter product lead 
time (less than a month vs. a key competitor at 4 months, see here).  
Going into 4Q26, we expect revenue to reach NT$11,425mn (-1% 
QoQ,+26% YoY), as we expect solid YoY across nearly all end 
markets. Rising raw material cost remains a key risk, while the

### Page 3

company continues to maintain a stable pricing strategy within the China market (which 
accounts for 90%+ of total revenue); thus we expect GM to slightly decline to 48.7% 
(-0.7ppt QoQ and +1.2ppt YoY). We continue to see the company’s ongoing effort in 
improving internal efficiency, which could partially offset the negative impact from rising 
material cost. 
We hold our conservative though optimistic view on the pneumatic industry outlook, as 
we continue to see downside risk in consumer electronics shipments (smartphone, etc) 
and the negative impact from rising raw material cost to persist over the coming 
quarters. We expect the company to continue to gain share in the China pneumatic 
market with its stable pricing strategy as well as its shift towards higher-quality 
pneumatic products.

Investment view 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we expect the company will continue to 
outperform the overall pneumatic market. We are positive on the company’s latest 
comment that the overall China pneumatic market YoY growth could be sustained at low 
single to mid single digit for a longer period, and management further mentioned that 
the company will outperform the market by 10ppt in 2027. This is mainly driven by the 
company’s latest guidance that nearly all end markets could see double digit YoY growth 
in 2027. While the company did mention the risk of decelerating YoY growth for the 
battery industry (which has seen solid YoY growth over the past two years), we expect 
solid growth from smaller segments, including machine tool/general 
machinery/textile/packaging, could offset the risk (we expect the 4 segments to account 
for 24% of total 2027E revenue vs. battery at 13%). 
We believe AirTAC is well positioned to overcome potential industry demand risks with 
its strong B/S and healthy cash flow position, which could further support market share 
gains during industry downturns (historically, AirTAC has accelerated its market share 
gains during industry downcycles, supported by its solid B/S and cash flow 
management). We also expect the company’s ROE to continue to improve in the coming 
years (ROE should improve to 26.2% in 2028E per GSe vs. 16%-17% in 2022-25) 
supporting a high dividend payout ratio (50%-60%+ per year per GSe). 
As a result, we maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC with unchanged 12m TP of NT$1,895.

### Page 4

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 1/1% to factor in the 
better-than-expected growth in the China pneumatic market. We revise up our 2026E 
OPM by 0.3ppt to factor in actual 3Q26 results and revise up our 2027/28E OPM by 
0.3/0.1ppt as we continue to expect the company to focus on enhancing internal 
efficiency. We revise down our 2026E EPS estimates by 4% after factoring in a higher tax 
rate in 2H26. 
Also, we introduce our 2029E earnings estimates in this report. 
Maintain our Buy rating and 12m TP of NT$1,895, which is based on unchanged P/E of 
26x on our 2027E EPS, as we believe the company could trade at 0.25x STDV above the 
average PE multiple during the previous pneumatic upcycle.

### Page 6

Investment Thesis - AirTAC
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC

continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring.

Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC.
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on

our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle).
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in
mainland China.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

Grand Process Technology Corp. (3131.TWO): SoIC / PLP to support 
future GM enhancement; 3Q26 GM miss
3 November 2026 | 10:54PM HKT
GPTC reported its 3Q26 preliminary results. Revenues was pre-announced, while GM 
came in at 36.6% (down 2.3ppt QoQ; down 4.2ppt YoY), below GSe/cons. of 
39.7%/40.6%, which we attribute to an unfavorable product mix. Opex ratio further 
improved to 14.6% (vs. 17.7% in 2Q26), better than our / Bloomberg consensus 
estimates of 15.1% / 16.3%, leading OP income to +34% QoQ, or +54% YoY. Overall, 
the miss on GM leads EPS miss our / Bloomberg consensus by 9% / 14%. Despite 
3Q26 GM miss, we believe GPTC’s multi-year growth outlook remains intact, with 
continuous capacity increase to better capture the demand from CoWoS capacity 
expansion, along with product mix upgrade toward SoIC and PLP , carrying higher 
content value. Maintain Buy.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut EPS by 4% / 2% / 0% in 
2026-28E, mainly on lower GM, as we slowdown the pace of product mix upgrade 
toward SoIC and PLP . Despite the cut, we continue to expect GM sequential 
enhancement, with 4Q26 GM up to 37.7% (vs. 36.6% in 3Q26) and up to 40.2% / 
42.5% in 2027-28E, driven by rising SoIC and PLP contribution.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

## Document 222: [005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업

(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

- Overweight(비중확대)

- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소)

- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

TUẦN 26-30/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản 
ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có m ức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 324: EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
flat on a monthly basis, bringing the annual rate up to 2.1% (up 0.4 pp).
Core CPI (excl. fresh food) matched it at 2.1% y/y (up 0.4 pp), in line
with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips
out fresh food and energy, rose sharply to 2.4% y/y (up 0.5 pp), its
fastest pace since January.
EMERGING ECONOMIES

ASIA
China
New monetary policy tool and export controls. The central bank’s Go-
vernor Pan Gongsheng unveiled a plan to switch (“when appropriate”)
its key policy rate from the 7-day reverse repo rate (at 1.4% since May
2025) to the overnight reverse repo rate, in order to reflect develop -
ments in the interbank market. New export controls: The government
plans to add 40 key technologies to the list of technologies prohibited
or restricted for export, including in the AI (for ex. LLMs, computing

clusters), biotechnology and photovoltaic sectors.
Indonesia
BI kept policy rate unchanged at 5.75%, raised its deposit facility rate
by 25bp to 5% and announced a phased cut in reserve requirements
from 9% to 7% by December. This combination of limited FX support
and measures to support credit growth sends mixed signals amidst
pressures on the rupiah following MSCI’s downgrade warning and un-
dermines the central bank’s credibility. The rupiah has lost 1% against

the USD since mid-October, with little contagion effect on other Asian
currencies.
South Korea
The Bank of Korea (BoK) kept its policy rate unchanged at 3.0%, but
the tightening cycle may not be over. CPI inflation was 2.9% y/y in Sep-
tember, above the central bank’s target of 2%, while high oil prices,
rapid growth in Seoul housing prices and wage pressures (though
concentrated in the electronics sector) raise the risk of lasting infla -
tion pressure. As economic activity remains robust, supported by strong

exports and recovering investment, the BoK has scope to raise rates
further.
EMEA
Hungary
Monetary status quo and 2027 budget in consolidation mode. The
Central Bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 5.5%, as expected. A cau-
tious stance will likely prevail in the short term. Inflation has been
picking up in recent months, albeit mildly, and will continue to move
upward as the government recently removed the margin cap on fuel.
Separately, the Budget for 2027 and the medium-term fiscal plan show

Hungary’s goal to join the Eurozone; the government expects to reduce
the fiscal deficit to 4.9% of GDP in 2027 (from 7% expected in 2026) and
towards 2.8% of GDP by 2030.
Türkiye
Cautious monetary loosening. The Central Bank lowered its policy rate
by 100 bp to 36% at its October Monetary Policy Committee meeting,
in line with market expectations. The overnight lending and borrowing
rates were also reduced by 100 bp, to 39% and 34.5%, respectively.

The improvement in underlying inflation has yet to translate into a
sustained period of low inflation, justifying the CBRT’s preference for a
measured pace of monetary easing.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
President Lula da Silva was re-elected on Sunday for a fourth term
(with 51.8% of the vote), defeating far right-wing candidate Flavio Bol-
sonaro (48.2%). Lula will enter this new term facing significant econo -
mic and political challenges. Public debt has risen to more than 82% of

GDP , while high borrowing costs and rigid mandatory spending leave
limited room to reconcile fiscal consolidation with social spending and
investment. Restoring confidence in the fiscal outlook will be a key
challenge in the short term.
COMMODITIES
Pressure rising. Amid growing rumours of imminent re-escalation of
military operations in the Gulf, energy prices rose sharply this week,
with brent touching $117/b – near its April peak, and European gas

prices (TTF) rising to 97 €/MWh on Monday, their highest level since
Dec 2022. The Asian gas benchmark (JKM) is following the same trend
(4.6% w/w to 31.4 $/mbtu, equivalent to around 96 €/MWh). Diesel
crack spreads are slightly decreasing in Europe (-2.8% w/w on Monday)

## Document 383: Open mouth operations (2026-11-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-11-07).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing 
AI concentration risk through a Total

Sylvain Huard

Hannah Chew

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.
